        {
            "id": "1daafd55-7cc2-4041-8bbc-214d69f95617",
            "author": {
                "role": "assistant",
                "name": null,
                "metadata": {}
            },
            "create_time": 1792071810.8940804,
            "update_time": 1792071835.809263,
            "content": {
                "content_type": "text",
                "parts": [
                    "To judge whether a ₹1.5 crore Noida property is a good investment over 10 years, we need to look at **the money you actually put in, the rent you keep after expenses, and what your sale proceeds will buy after inflation**.\n\nA property that appreciates from ₹1.5 crore to ₹3 crore can look like a great investment on paper. But stamp duty, registration, maintenance, vacant months, selling costs and inflation can reduce the real return considerably.\n\nI'll model three appreciation scenarios: 5%, 8% and 12% annually, assuming you buy without a loan and rent the property out for 10 years.\n\n## 1. Assumptions for the calculation\n\nI'll use a ₹1.5 crore purchase price and the following estimates. They're modelling assumptions, not quotes for a specific flat.\n\n| Cost or assumption | Amount |\n|---|---:|\n| Property purchase price | ₹1.50 Cr |\n| Stamp duty and registration | 8% = ₹12 lakh |\n| Other initial costs | ₹3 lakh |\n| **Total initial outlay** | **₹1.65 Cr** |\n| Starting gross rental yield | 3.9% |\n| Vacancy allowance | 1 month per year |\n| Maintenance and repairs | ₹1.2 lakh in year one |\n| Annual rent growth | 5% |\n| Selling expenses | 2% of sale price |\n| Inflation | 5% per year |\n\nThe 8% purchase-cost estimate is based on a commonly applied 7% stamp duty plus 1% registration charge in Uttar Pradesh; check the applicable rate and valuation basis using the <Entity category=\"organization\" value=\"Uttar Pradesh Stamp and Registration Department\" disambig=\"Government property registration authority\"/> calculator before purchasing. <Cite refs={[\"turn665610search0\",\"turn665610search2\"]}/>\n\nA 3.9% gross rental yield is consistent with a reported city-level Noida estimate for Q2 2026, although your particular flat could perform better or worse. <Cite refs={[\"turn665610search4\"]}/>\n\nFor simplicity, I assume maintenance and rent both rise by 5% annually. I exclude income tax, capital-gains tax, loan interest and major unexpected repairs. These could change the outcome.\n\n## 2. Your actual 10-year outcome\n\n<box border radius=\"xl\" padding={4} gap={4}>\n  <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"sm\">Total cash invested upfront</text>\n  <title size=\"2xl\">₹1.65 crore</title>\n  <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"sm\">Includes the property price and estimated purchase costs.</text>\n  <divider color=\"subtle\"/>\n  {#each [{rate:\"5%\",sale:\"₹2.44 Cr\",net:\"₹2.39 Cr\",nominal:\"₹2.92 Cr\",real:\"₹1.89 Cr\",profit:\"₹24 lakh\"},{rate:\"8%\",sale:\"₹3.24 Cr\",net:\"₹3.17 Cr\",nominal:\"₹3.70 Cr\",real:\"₹2.36 Cr\",profit:\"₹71 lakh\"},{rate:\"12%\",sale:\"₹4.66 Cr\",net:\"₹4.57 Cr\",nominal:\"₹5.09 Cr\",real:\"₹3.22 Cr\",profit:\"₹1.57 Cr\"}] as s}\n    <box gap={2}>\n      <row align=\"center\" justify=\"between\">\n        <title size=\"lg\">{s.rate} annual appreciation</title>\n        <badge>{s.profit} real gain</badge>\n      </row>\n      <grid columns={2} gap={2}>\n        <grid-item>\n          <box background=\"surface-secondary\" radius=\"md\" padding={3} gap={1}>\n            <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"xs\">Property value in year 10</text>\n            <title size=\"lg\">{s.sale}</title>\n          </box>\n        </grid-item>\n        <grid-item>\n          <box background=\"surface-secondary\" radius=\"md\" padding={3} gap={1}>\n            <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"xs\">Sale proceeds after 2% costs</text>\n            <title size=\"lg\">{s.net}</title>\n          </box>\n        </grid-item>\n        <grid-item>\n          <box padding={2} gap={1}>\n            <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"xs\">Sale proceeds + net rent, nominal</text>\n            <title>{s.nominal}</title>\n          </box>\n        </grid-item>\n        <grid-item>\n          <box padding={2} gap={1}>\n            <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"xs\">Same total in today's money</text>\n            <title>{s.real}</title>\n          </box>\n        </grid-item>\n      </grid>\n    </box>\n    {#if s.rate!==\"12%\"}<divider color=\"subtle\"/>{/if}\n  {/each}\n  <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"xs\">Rounded estimates. The real-value figures include the net rental income received over the decade, adjusted for inflation, plus net sale proceeds adjusted for inflation. They are compared against the ₹1.65 crore initial outlay.</text>\n</box>\n\nThe rental model produces approximately ₹52 lakh in cumulative net rent over 10 years before tax, assuming rent increases by 5% annually and maintenance costs rise at the same rate.\n\n## 3. Does it still look like a good investment?\n\n**At 5% annual appreciation, the investment looks fairly modest after costs and inflation. At 8%, it looks more attractive. At 12%, it performs very well, but that outcome depends on sustained strong appreciation.**\n\n<Chart content={{\"chartType\":\"bar\",\"meta\":{\"title\":\"Estimated gain after inflation\",\"description\":\"Total 10-year wealth gain over the ₹1.65 crore initial outlay, expressed in today's rupees\"},\"xKey\":\"scenario\",\"series\":[{\"dataKey\":\"gain\",\"label\":\"Real gain\",\"valuePrefix\":\"₹\",\"valueSuffix\":\" lakh\"}],\"yAxisMin\":0,\"data\":[{\"scenario\":\"5% growth\",\"gain\":24},{\"scenario\":\"8% growth\",\"gain\":71},{\"scenario\":\"12% growth\",\"gain\":157}]}}/>\n\nThese are simplified scenario results, not expected or guaranteed profits. In particular, tax treatment and unexpected capital expenditure could materially reduce your gain.\n\n## 4. What I would do before committing ₹1.5 crore\n\n- **Prioritise rental demand.** A flat that rents quickly and consistently reduces your dependence on price appreciation.\n- **Negotiate hard on the purchase price.** Paying ₹10–15 lakh too much at entry can erase several years of rent.\n- **Buy a completed, legally clear property.** Avoid paying a premium for infrastructure or amenities that may take years to materialise.\n- **Compare against alternatives.** A property investment should be assessed against what the same capital could earn elsewhere, after tax and risk.\n\nMy view: if you can buy a good flat in Sector 137 or Sector 75 at a sensible price, with a realistic gross yield near 4%, it can be a reasonable long-term investment. I would not buy in Sector 150 purely on the assumption of 12% annual appreciation.\n\nThe next useful step is to run this same calculation using **the actual asking price and expected rent of one specific society**. That will tell us much more than sector averages."
                ]
            },

                                "title": "Noida home prices soar 125%, Gurugram 117% since 2019; check property rates and rental yields across 11 cities",
                                "snippet": "Between 2019 and Q2 2026, property prices in Noida surged by 125%, increasing from ₹4,795 per square foot to ₹10,780. Gurugram also experienced a significant rise of 117% in the same period. Rental yields in Noida improved modestly from 3.2% in 2019 to 3.9% in Q2 2026. The report further highlights trends in property prices and rent increases across 11 Indian cities, including notable markets like Delhi NCR, Bengaluru, Hyderabad, and Mumbai. These findings reflect a broader nationwide trend of rising property values and rental yields in major urban centers, driven by urban development and high demand.",
